## Operations



BP [North Sea](21179.md) Headquarters in [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen), built by the Bowmer and Kirkland group at a cost of £50 million

As of 31 December 2018, BP had operations in 78 countries worldwide[^cite_note-AR18-glance-202] with the global headquarters in London, United Kingdom. BP operations are organized into three business segments, [Upstream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upstream_\(petroleum_industry\)), [Downstream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downstream_\(petroleum_industry\)), and [renewables](25784.md).[^cite_note-AR18-segments-203]

Since 1951, BP has annually published its *Statistical Review of [World Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Energy)*, which is considered an energy industry benchmark.[^cite_note-reuters120613-204]

### Operations by location

#### United Kingdom



The BP chemicals plant in [Saltend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_End) near [Hull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston_upon_Hull), England

BP has a major corporate campus in [Sunbury-on-Thames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunbury-on-Thames) which is home to around 3,500 employees and over 50 business units.[^cite_note-BP-Sunbury-205] Its [North Sea](21179.md) operations are headquartered in [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen), [Scotland](26994.md). BP's trading functions are based at [20 Canada Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20_Canada_Square) in [Canary Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canary_Wharf), London. BP has three major research and development centres in the UK.[^cite_note-commons2012-206]

As of 2020, and following the sale of its Andrew and Shearwater interests, BP's operations were focussed in the [Clair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clair_oilfield), Quad 204 and [ETAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Trough_Area_Project) hubs.[^cite_note-207] In 2011, the company announced that it is focusing its investment in the UK North Sea into four development projects including the Clair, Devenick, [Schiehallion and Loyal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schiehallion_oilfield), and Kinnoull oilfields.[^cite_note-Swint2011-208] BP is the operator of the Clair oilfield, which has been appraised as the largest hydrocarbon resource in the UK.[^cite_note-Bawden2011-209]

There are 1,200 BP service stations in the UK.[^cite_note-Gosden16-210][^cite_note-BP-UK-211] Since 2018 BP operates the UK's largest [electric vehicle](279350.md) charging network through its subsidiary [BP Pulse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BP_Pulse) (formerly Chargemaster).[^cite_note-AR18-glance-202]

In February 2020, BP announced a joint venture with [EnBW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EnBW) to develop and operate 3 GW of offshore wind capacity in the [Crown Estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Estate) Leasing Round 4.[^cite_note-212] This is BP's first move into [Britain's offshore wind market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_offshore_wind_farms_in_the_United_Kingdom), however, BP currently provides a range of services to the offshore wind sector in the UK through its subsidiary ONYX InSight who provide a range of [predictive maintenance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predictive_maintenance) and engineering consultancy services to the sector.[^cite_note-213]

In February 2022, BP announced it acquired a 30% stake in the [London](17867.md)-based company, Green Biofuels Ltd, a producer of [renewable hydrogenated vegetable oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrogenated_vegetable_oil) fuels that can be used as a direct replacement for diesel.[^cite_note-214]

#### United States



The headquarters of BP America in [Westlake Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westlake_Park_\(Houston\)), [Houston](13774.md)



The *[Thunder Horse PDQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thunder_Horse_PDQ)* semi-submersible oil platform in the [Thunder Horse Oil Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thunder_Horse_Oil_Field)

The United States operations comprise nearly one-third of BP's operations.[^cite_note-NYTBusiness2012-215] BP employs approximately 14,000 people in the United States.[^cite_note-AR18-USlabour-216] In 2018, BP's total production in the United States included 385,000 barrels per day (61,200 m<sup>3</sup>/d) of oil and 1.9 billion cubic feet per day (54 million cubic metres per day) of natural gas,[^cite_note-AR18-USproduction-217] and its refinery throughput was 703,000 barrels per day (111,800 m<sup>3</sup>/d).[^cite_note-AR18-USrefinery-218]

BP's major subsidiary in the United States is BP America, Inc. (formerly: Standard Oil Company (Ohio) and [Sohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sohio)) based in [Houston, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston,_Texas).[^cite_note-biz030513-219] BP Exploration & Production Inc., a 1996 established Houston-based subsidiary, is dealing with oil exploration and production.[^cite_note-bpexploration-220] BP Corporation North America, Inc., provides petroleum refining services as also transportation fuel, heat and light energy.[^cite_note-bpcorporation-221] BP Products North America, Inc., a 1954 established Houston-based subsidiary, is engaged in the exploration, development, production, refining, and marketing of oil and natural gas.[^cite_note-bpproduct-222] BP America Production Company, a [New Mexico](21649.md)-based subsidiary, engages in oil and gas exploration and development.[^cite_note-bpproduction-223] BP Energy Company, a Houston-based subsidiary, is a provider of natural gas, power, and risk management services to the industrial and utility sectors and a retail electric provider in Texas.[^cite_note-bpenergy-224]

BP's upstream activities in the Lower 48 states are conducted through Denver-based BPX Energy.[^cite_note-denver040220-149] It has a 7.5 billion barrels (1.19 billion cubic metres) resource base on 5.7 million acres (2,300,000 ha).[^cite_note-OGJ15-225] It has [shale positions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_gas_in_the_United_States) in the [Woodford, Oklahoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_gas_in_the_United_States#Woodford_Shale,_Oklahoma), [Haynesville, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_gas_in_the_United_States#Haynesville_Shale,_Louisiana), and [Eagle Ford, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_Ford_Formation) shales.[^cite_note-Pulsinelli2014-226][^cite_note-Rouan2012-227] It has [unconventional gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unconventional_gas) ([shale gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shale_gas) or [tight gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tight_gas)) stakes also in Colorado, [New Mexico](21649.md) and Wyoming, primarily in the [San Juan Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Juan_Basin).[^cite_note-Hasterok2012-228][^cite_note-ZacksJun2012-229][^cite_note-BPUSExploration-230]

As of 2019, BP produced about 300,000 barrels per day (48,000 m<sup>3</sup>/d) of oil equivalent in the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md).[^cite_note-Ault19-231] BP operates the [Atlantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis_oil_field), [Mad Dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mad_Dog_oil_field), Na Kika, and [Thunder Horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thunder_Horse_oil_field) production platforms while holding interest in hubs operated by other companies.[^cite_note-Ambrose17-232][^cite_note-BP-Gulf-233] In April 2023, BP launched a new oil rig, the *Argos*, in the Gulf.[^cite_note-234]

BP operates [Whiting Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whiting_Refinery) in Indiana[^cite_note-BPUSRefining-235] and [Cherry Point Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry_Point_Refinery) in Washington.[^cite_note-BPUSRefining-235] It formerly co-owned and operated a refinery in Toledo, Ohio, with [Husky Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Husky_Energy), but sold its stake in the refinery in February 2023 to [Cenovus Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cenovus_Energy).[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238]

BP operates nine onshore [wind farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_farms) in six states, and held an interest in another in [Hawaii](13270.md) with a net generating capacity of 1,679 MW.[^cite_note-BP-wind-239] These wind farms include the [Cedar Creek 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedar_Creek_Wind_Farm), [Titan 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titan_Wind_Project), Goshen North, [Flat Ridge 1 and 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_Ridge_Wind_Farm), Mehoopany, [Fowler Ridge 1, 2 and 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fowler_Ridge_Wind_Farm) and Auwahi wind farms.[^cite_note-BP-wind-sites-240] It is also in process to acquire 50% non-operating stake in the Empire Wind off New York and Beacon Wind off Massachusetts offshore wind farms.[^cite_note-reuters100920-163]

#### Other locations

In Egypt, BP produces approximately 15% of the country's total oil production and 40% of its domestic gas.[^cite_note-Petzet2012-241] The company also has offshore gas developments in the East Nile Delta Mediterranean, and in the West Nile Delta,[^cite_note-ZacksJune2012-242] where the company has a joint investment of US\$9 billion with [Wintershall Dea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wintershall_Dea) to develop North Alexandria and West Mediterranean concessions offshore gas fields.[^cite_note-Hromadko2010-243][^cite_note-BPWestNile2010-244]

BP is active in offshore oil development in Angola, where it holds an interest in a total of nine oil exploration and production blocks covering more than 30,000 square kilometres (12,000 sq mi). This includes four blocks it acquired in December 2011 and an additional block that is operated by Brazilian national oil company, [Petrobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrobras), in which it holds a 40% stake.[^cite_note-BPAngolaAcquisition-245]

BP has a stake in exploration of two blocks of offshore deepwater assets in the South China Sea.[^cite_note-China2012-246][^cite_note-Xu2012-247]

In India, BP owns a 30% share of oil and gas assets operated by [Reliance Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reliance_Industries), including exploration and production rights in more than 20 offshore oil and gas blocks, representing an investment of more than US\$7 billion into oil and gas exploration in the country.[^cite_note-Sharma2011-248]

BP has major liquefied natural gas activities in Indonesia, where it operates the [Tangguh LNG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangguh_gas_field) project, which began production in 2009 and has a capacity of 7.6 million tonnes of liquid natural gas per year.[^cite_note-Grieder2009-249] In 2025, BP signed three additional production sharing contracts for the Bintuni, Drawa, and Barong blocks, increasing the company's total participation in Indonesia to 11 oil and gas blocks.[^cite_note-o\&g-250] Also in that country, the company has invested in the exploration and development of [coalbed methane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalbed_methane).[^cite_note-Suhana2011-251]

BP operates in Iraq as part of the joint venture [Rumaila Operating Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rumaila_Field_Operating_Organization) in the [Rumaila oil field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rumaila_oil_field), the world's fourth largest oilfield, where it produced over 1 million barrels per day (160×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>3</sup> m<sup>3</sup>/d) of oil equivalent in 2011.[^cite_note-ReutersOct2009-252][^cite_note-UPIJan11-253] A BBC investigation found in 2022 that waste-gas was being burned as close as 350 meters from people's homes. A leaked report from [Ministry of Health (Iraq)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Iraq\)) blamed [air pollution](10934212.md) for 20% rise in [cancer](105219.md) in [Basra](57580.md) between 2015 and 2018.[^cite_note-BBC1-254] The Iraqi Ministry of Health has banned its employees from speaking about the health damage.[^cite_note-BBC1-254] Iraqi Environment Minister Jassem al-Falahi later admitted that "pollution from oil production is the main reason for increases in local cancer rates."[^cite_note-255] In February 2025, BP signed a \$25 billion deal to redevelop four oil and gas fields in [Kirkuk](199292.md), Iraq.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257]

In Oman, BP currently has a 60% participation interest in Block 61. Block 61 is one of Oman's largest gas blocks with a daily production capacity of 1.5 billion cubic feet of gas and more than 65,000 barrels of condensate. It covers around 3,950 km in central Oman and contains the largest tight gas development in the Middle East. On 1 February 2021, BP inked a deal to sell 20% participating interest in Block 61 to Thailand's [PTT Exploration and Production Public Company Ltd. (PTTEP)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PTT_Exploration_and_Production) for a total of \$2.6 billion. Upon closure of the sale, the BP will remain the block's operator with a 40% interest.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]



A BP [road train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_train) in the Australian outback

Prior to March 2021, BP operated the [Kwinana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwinana_Oil_Refinery) refinery in [Western Australia](33613.md), which was the country's largest refinery,[^cite_note-Denton15-260] supplying about 70% of Western Australia's fuel needs. The former refinery, located on [Cockburn Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cockburn_Sound), was [converted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwinana_Oil_Refinery#Conversion_to_import_terminal) to an import terminal and, as of 2025, was transitioning into a [biorefinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biorefinery) and green hydrogen production facility.[^cite_note-261] But in 2025 BP announced it was ending its role in producing hydrogen in the region.[^cite_note-262]

BP is a non-operating joint venture partner in the [North West Shelf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_West_Shelf), which produces LNG, pipeline gas, condensate and oil.[^cite_note-Chambers16-263] The NWS venture is Australia's largest resource development and accounts for around one third of Australia's oil and gas production.[^cite_note-Lanis17-264][^cite_note-Daiss16-265]

BP operates the two largest oil and gas production projects in the Azerbaijan's sector of the Caspian Sea, the [Azeri–Chirag–Guneshli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azeri%E2%80%93Chirag%E2%80%93Guneshli) offshore oil fields, which supplies 80% of the country's oil production, and the [Shah Deniz gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Deniz_gas_field). It also and develops the [Shafag-Asiman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shafag-Asiman) complex of offshore geological structures.[^cite_note-Neff2010-266][^cite_note-Yevgrashina2012-267][^cite_note-today-268] In addition, it operates the [Sangachal terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangachal_Terminal) and the Azerbaijan's major export pipelines through [Georgia](48768.md) such as [Baku–Tbilisi–Ceyhan](397567.md), [Baku–Supsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku%E2%80%93Supsa_Pipeline) and [South Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus_Pipeline) pipelines.[^cite_note-269]



A GDH (subsidiary of BP) oil depot, Frontigan, Hérault, France

BP's refining operations in continental Europe include Europe's second-largest oil refinery, located in Rotterdam, the Netherlands, which can process up to 377,000 barrels (59,900 m<sup>3</sup>) of crude oil per day.[^cite_note-Reuters2009-270] Other facilities are located in Ingolstadt, Gelsenkirchen and Lingen, in Germany, as well as one in Castellón, Spain.[^cite_note-271]

In addition to its offshore operations in the British zone of North Sea, BP has interests in the Norwegian section of the sea through its stake in Aker BP. As of December 2018, BP holds a 19.75% stake in Russia's state-controlled oil company Rosneft.[^cite_note-\:2-129][^cite_note-reuters220313-131][^cite_note-AR18-Rosneft-272]

Retail operations of motor vehicle fuels in Europe are present in the United Kingdom, France, Germany (through the Aral brand), the Netherlands, Switzerland, Italy, Austria, Poland, Portugal, Greece and Turkey.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275]

BP's Canadian operations are headquartered in [Calgary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calgary) and the company operates primarily in [Newfoundland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newfoundland).[^cite_note-276] It purchases crude oil for the company's refineries in the United States, and has a 35 per cent stake in the undeveloped Bay du Nord project and three offshore exploration block in [Newfoundland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newfoundland).[^cite_note-upstream1611112-277]

BP is the largest oil and gas producer in [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md), where it holds more than 1,350 square kilometres (520 sq mi) of offshore assets and is the largest shareholder in [Atlantic LNG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_LNG), one of the largest [LNG plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LNG_plant) in [Western Hemisphere](73162.md).[^cite_note-UPIMay11-278]

In Brazil, BP holds stakes in offshore oil and gas exploration in the Barreirinhas, Ceará and [Campos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campos_Basin) basins, in addition to onshore processing facilities.[^cite_note-Flynn2012-279] BP also operates biofuel production facilities in Brazil, including three cane sugar mills for ethanol production.[^cite_note-Fick2011-280][^cite_note-reuters140911-281]

BP operated in Singapore until 2004 when it sold its retail network of 28 stations and LPG business to [Singapore Petroleum Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Petroleum_Company) (SPC). It also sold its 50% in SPC.[^cite_note-282]

BP has a significant presence in [South Africa](17416221.md), operating refineries and 500 gas stations across the country.[^cite_note-Shell_kisses_South_African_service_stations_and_refinery_goodbye-283]

### Exploration and production

See also: [Oil fields operated by BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_fields_operated_by_BP)

BP Upstream's activities include exploring for new oil and natural gas resources, developing access to such resources, and producing, transporting, storing and processing oil and natural gas.[^cite_note-Forbes2012-284][^cite_note-CNNMoney2011-285] The activities in this area of operations take place in 25 countries worldwide. In 2018, BP produced around 3.7 million barrels per day (590×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>3</sup> m<sup>3</sup>/d) of oil equivalent,[^cite_note-AR18-glance-202] of which 2.191 million barrels per day (348.3×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>3</sup> m<sup>3</sup>/d) were liquids and 8.659 billion cubic feet per day (245.2 million cubic metres per day) was natural gas, and had total proved reserves of 19,945 million barrels (3,171.0×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>6</sup> m<sup>3</sup>) of oil equivalent, of which liquids accounted 11,456 million barrels (1,821.4×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>6</sup> m<sup>3</sup>) barrels and natural gas 49.239 trillion cubic feet (1.3943 trillion cubic metres).[^cite_note-AR18-estimates-286] In addition to the conventional oil exploration and production, BP has a stake in the three oil sands projects in Canada.[^cite_note-fp300712-112][^cite_note-reuters240620-287]

BP had plans to drop its oil and gas production to fall by at least one million barrels a day by 2030, a 40% reduction on 2019 levels, but this was scrapped in October 2024, with plans for an increase in production instead.[^cite_note-\:4-288][^cite_note-cnn040820-289]

### Refining and marketing



An [Aral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aral_AG) service station in Weiterstadt, Germany

BP downstream's activities include the refining, marketing, manufacturing, transportation, trading and supply of [crude oil](23195.md) and [petroleum products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleum_product).[^cite_note-Forbes2012-284] Downstream is responsible for BP's fuels and lubricants businesses, and has major operations located in Europe, North America and Asia.[^cite_note-ReutersProfile2012-290] As of 2018, BP owned or had a share in 11 refineries.[^cite_note-AR18-USrefinery-218]

BP, which employs about 1,800 people in oil trading and trades over 5 million barrels per day (790×1&#x30;~~^~~<sup>3</sup> m<sup>3</sup>/d) of oil and refined products, is the world's third-biggest oil trader after [Royal Dutch Shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Dutch_Shell) and [Vitol](17518274.md).[^cite_note-Reuters_BP_trading-291] The operation is estimated to be able to generate over \$1 billion trading profits in a good year.[^cite_note-Reuters_BP_trading-291]

[Air BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_BP) is the aviation division of BP, providing [aviation fuel](642298.md), lubricants & services. It has operations in over 50 countries worldwide. [BP Shipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BP_Shipping) provides the logistics to move BP's oil and gas cargoes to market, as well as marine structural assurance.[^cite_note-292] It manages a large fleet of vessels most of which are held on long-term operating leases. BP Shipping's chartering teams based in London, Singapore, and Chicago also charter third party vessels on both time charter and voyage charter basis. The BP-managed fleet consists of [Very Large Crude Carriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Very_Large_Crude_Carrier) (VLCCs), one North Sea shuttle tanker, medium size crude and product carriers, [liquefied natural gas (LNG) carriers](12378960.md), [liquefied petroleum gas (LPG) carriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LPG_carrier), and coasters. All of these ships are double-hulled.[^cite_note-293]

BP has around 18,700 service stations worldwide.[^cite_note-AR18-glance-202] Its flagship retail brand is BP Connect, a chain of service stations combined with a convenience store,[^cite_note-BPServiceStations-294] although in the US it is gradually being transitioned to the [ampm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ampm) format. BP also owns half of Kentucky-based convenience store company [Thorntons LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thorntons_LLC) with ArcLight Capital Partners (who own the Gulf brand in the United States) since 2019. On 13 July 2021, BP announced it will take acquire ArcLight Capital Partners' share of Thorntons, and thus fully own the convenience store company. The deal is expected to close later in the year.[^cite_note-295] In Germany and Luxembourg, BP operates service stations under the [Aral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aral_AG) brand.[^cite_note-BPAral-94] On the US West Coast, in the states of California, Oregon, Washington, Nevada, Idaho, Arizona, and Utah, BP primarily operates service stations under the ARCO brand.[^cite_note-BPArco-296] In Australia BP operates a number of BP Travel Centres, large-scale destination sites located which, in addition to the usual facilities in a BP Connect site, also feature food-retail tenants such as [McDonald's](2480627.md), [KFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KFC) and [Nando's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nando's) and facilities for long-haul truck drivers.[^cite_note-BP_Truck_Stop_Australia-297]

[Castrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castrol) is BP's main brand for industrial and [automotive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive) [lubricants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lubricants) and is applied to a large range of BP oils, [greases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grease_\(lubricant\)) and similar products for most [lubrication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lubrication) applications.[^cite_note-BPCastrol-298]

### Clean energy



A BP photovoltaic (PV) module that is composed of multiple PV cells. Two or more interconnected PV modules create an array.



The [Fowler Ridge Wind Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fowler_Ridge_Wind_Farm)

BP's public rhetoric and pledges emphasise that the company is shifting towards climate-friendly, low-carbon and transition strategies. However, a 2022 study found that the company's spending on clean energy was insignificant and opaque, with little to suggest that the company's discourse matched its actions.[^cite_note-299]

BP was the first of [supermajors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermajor) to say that it would focus on energy sources other than fossil fuels.[^cite_note-reuters151217-146] It established an [alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_energy) and low carbon energy business in 2005. According to the company, it spent a total of \$8.3 billion in renewable energy projects including solar, wind, and [biofuels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biofuels), and non-renewable projects including [natural gas](22131.md) and [hydrogen power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrogen_power), through completion in 2013.[^cite_note-Schiller-2012-300][^cite_note-Wells2012-301][^cite_note-sieber151217-302] The relatively small size of BP's alternative energy operations has led to allegations of [greenwashing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwashing) by [Greenpeace](12233.md),[^cite_note-greenpeace211010-303] [Mother Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Jones_\(magazine\)),[^cite_note-motherjones040510-304] and energy analyst and activist [Antonia Juhasz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonia_Juhasz),[^cite_note-accuracy210812-305] among others.[^cite_note-guardian201108-306] In 2018, the CEO Bob Dudley said that out of the company's total spending of \$15 to \$17 billion per year, about \$500 million will be invested in low-carbon energy and technology.[^cite_note-reuters210518-307] In August 2020, BP promised to increase its annual low carbon investments to \$5 billion by 2030.[^cite_note-cnn040820-289] The company announced plans to transform into an integrated energy company, with a renewed focus on investing away from oil and into low-carbon technologies.[^cite_note-308] It has set targets to have a renewables portfolio of 20 GW by 2025, and 50 GW by 2030.[^cite_note-309]

BP operates nine wind farms in seven states of the U.S., and held an interest in another in [Hawaii](13270.md) with a net generating capacity of 1,679 MW.[^cite_note-BP-wind-239] It is also in process to acquire 50% non-operating stake in the Empire Wind off New York and Beacon Wind off Massachusetts offshore wind farms.[^cite_note-reuters100920-163] BP and [Tesla, Inc.](5533631.md) are cooperating for testing the energy storage by battery at the Titan 1 wind farm.[^cite_note-AR18-wind-310] BP Launchpad has also invested in ONYX InSight, one of the leading providers of predictive analytic solutions serving the wind industry.[^cite_note-energyvoice230120-311]

In Brazil, BP owns two ethanol producers—*Companhia Nacional de Açúcar e Álcool* and*Tropical BioEnergia*—with three ethanol mills.[^cite_note-reuters140911-281] These mills produce around 800,000 cubic metres per annum (5,000,000 bbl/a) of ethanol equivalent.[^cite_note-spw201217-312] BP has invested in an agricultural biotechnology company Chromatin, a company developing crops that can grow on marginal land and that are optimized to be used as feedstock for biofuel.[^cite_note-Herndon2011-313] Its joint venture with DuPont called Butamax, which has developed the patented bio-butanol-producing technology,[^cite_note-delaware280815-314] and owns an isobutanol plant in [Scandia, Kansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandia,_Kansas), United States.[^cite_note-biomass030417-148] In addition BP owns biomethane production facilities in [Canton, Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canton,_Michigan), and North Shelby, Tennessee, as well as share of facilities under construction in [Oklahoma City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oklahoma_City) and [Atlanta](3138.md).[^cite_note-Hardcastle17-147] BP's subsidiary Air BP supplies aviation biofuel at [Oslo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Airport,_Gardermoen), [Halmstad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halmstad_Airport), and [Bergen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_Airport,_Flesland) airports.[^cite_note-aviationpross210817-315]

BP owns a 43% stake in [Lightsource BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lightsource_BP), a company which focuses on the managing and maintaining [solar farms](34779206.md). As of 2017, Lightsource has commissioned 1.3 GW of solar capacity and manages about 2 GW of solar capacity. It plans to increase the capacity up to 8 GW through projects in the United States, India, Europe and the Middle East.[^cite_note-bloomberg151217-145][^cite_note-reuters151217-146] BP has invested \$20 million in Israeli quick-charging battery firm StoreDot Ltd.[^cite_note-bloomberg240518-316] It operates electric vehicle charging networks in the UK under its subsidiary [BP Chargemaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BP_Chargemaster), and in China via a joint venture with [Didi Chuxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didi_Chuxing).[^cite_note-icis141020-153]

In partnership with [Ørsted A/S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%98rsted_\(company\)), BP plans a 50 MV [electrolyser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrolyser) at the Lingen refinery to produce hydrogen using North Sea wind power. Production is expected to begin in 2024.[^cite_note-ft101120-317]

BP is a majority shareholder in carbon offset developer Finite Carbon,[^cite_note-reuters161220-164] and acquired 9 GW of US solar projects in 2021.[^cite_note-318]

In 2023, following the announcement of record profits, the company scaled back their emissions targets. Originally, the company promised a 35-40% cut of emissions by the end of the decade. On 7 February, BP revised the target to a 20-30% cut in emissions, stating that it needed to keep up with the current demands for oil and gas.[^cite_note-319]

### Stock

The company's shares are primarily traded on the London Stock Exchange, but also listed on the Frankfurt Stock Exchange in Germany. In the United States shares are traded in US\$ on the New York Stock Exchange in the form of [American depository shares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_depositary_receipt) (ADS). One ADS represents six ordinary shares.[^cite_note-BPShare-328]

Following the United States [Federal Trade Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Trade_Commission)'s approval of the BP-Amoco merger in 1998, Amoco's stock was removed from the [S\&P 500](170992.md) and was merged with BP shares on the [London Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Stock_Exchange).[^cite_note-BBC1998-329]

### Branding and public relations

In the first quarter of 2001 the company adopted the marketing name of BP, and replaced its "Green Shield" logo with the "Helios" symbol, a green and yellow sunflower logo named after the [Greek sun god](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios) and designed to represent energy in its many forms. BP introduced a new corporate slogan – "Beyond Petroleum" along with a \$200M advertising and marketing campaign.[^cite_note-Ries-330][^cite_note-Landman-331] According to the company, the new slogan represented their focus on meeting the growing demand for fossil fuels, manufacturing and delivering more advanced products, and to enable transitioning to a lower [carbon footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_footprint).[^cite_note-332]

By 2008, BP's branding campaign had succeeded with the culmination of a 2007 Effie Award from the [American Marketing Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Marketing_Association), and consumers had the impression that BP was one of the greenest petroleum companies in the world.[^cite_note-333] BP was criticised by environmentalists and marketing experts, who stated that the company's alternative energy activities were only a fraction of the company's business at the time.[^cite_note-334] According to [Democracy Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_Now), BP's marketing campaign amounted to a deceptive [greenwashing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwashing) public-relations [spin campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_\(public_relations\)) given that BP's 2008 budget included more than \$20 billion for fossil fuel investment and less than \$1.5 billion for all alternative forms of energy.[^cite_note-Amy_Goodman-335][^cite_note-Kempff-336] Oil and energy analyst [Antonia Juhasz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonia_Juhasz) notes BP's investment in green technologies peaked at 4% of its exploratory budget prior to cutbacks, including the discontinuation of BP Solar and the closure of its alternative energy headquarters in London.[^cite_note-reuters211211-133][^cite_note-Amy_Goodman-335] According to Juhasz, "four percent...hardly qualifies the company to be Beyond Petroleum", citing BP's "aggressive modes of production, whether it's the tar sands \[or] offshore".[^cite_note-Amy_Goodman-335]

BP attained a negative public image from the series of industrial accidents that occurred through the 2000s, and its public image was severely damaged after the Deepwater Horizon explosion and Gulf Oil spill. In the immediate aftermath of the spill, [BP initially downplayed the severity of the incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_Deepwater_Horizon_oil_spill#BP_public_relations), and made many of the same PR errors that Exxon had made after the *Exxon Valdez* disaster.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-Wired-338] CEO Tony Hayward was criticised for his statements and had committed several gaffes, including stating that he "wanted his life back."[^cite_note-339] Some in the media commended BP for some of its social media efforts, such as the use of Twitter and Facebook as well as a section of the company's website where it communicated its efforts to clean up the spill.[^cite_note-Slate2010-340][^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342]

In February 2012 BP North America launched a \$500 million branding campaign to rebuild its brand.[^cite_note-343]

The company's advertising budget was about \$5 million per week during the four-month spill in the Gulf of Mexico, totalling nearly \$100 million.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345]

In May 2012, BP tasked a press office staff member to openly join discussions on the Wikipedia article's talk page and suggest content to be posted by other editors.[^cite_note-346] Controversy emerged in 2013 over the amount of content from BP that had entered this article.[^cite_note-zdnet2013-347][^cite_note-348] Wikipedia co-founder [Jimmy Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Wales) stated that, by identifying himself as a BP staff member, the contributor in question had complied with site policy regarding conflicts of interest.[^cite_note-zdnet2013-347]

### Integrity and compliance

Investigative journalism by [BBC Panorama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panorama_\(TV_programme\)) and Africa Eye aired in June 2019 criticising BP for the way in which it had obtained the development rights of Cayar Offshore Profond and St. Louis Offshore Profond blocks, off the coast of [Senegal](27278.md) in 2017. In 2012, a [Frank Timiș](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Timi%C8%99) company, Petro-Tim, though previously unknown to the oil industry, was awarded a license to explore the blocks despite having no known record in the industry. Soon after, Aliou Sall, brother of Senegal's president, [Macky Sall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macky_Sall), was hired at the company, implying a conflict of interest,[^cite_note-\:1-349] causing public outrage in Senegal. The 2019 program by BBC Panorama and Africa Eye accuses BP of a failure in [due diligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Due_diligence) when it agreed on a deal with Timis Corporation in 2017. The deal by BP is expected to provide substantial royalties to Frank Timiș despite accusations of initially obtaining the exploration rights through corruption. [Kosmos Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosmos_Energy) was also implicated.[^cite_note-350] BP refutes any implications of improper conduct. Regarding the acquisition of Timis Corporation interests in Senegal in April 2017, BP states that it "paid what it considered a fair market value for the interests at this stage of exploration/development". However, BP has not made public what was the basis of the valuation, and states that "the details of the deal are confidential".[^cite_note-351] BP argues that "the amount which would be paid separately by BP to Timis Corporation would be less than one percent of what the Republic of Senegal would receive". Senegal's justice ministry has called an inquiry into the energy contracts.[^cite_note-\:1-349]

### LGBTQ recognition

In 2014, BP backed a global study researching challenges for lesbian, gay, bisexual and transgender employees and for ways that companies can be a "force for change" for LGBT workers around the world.[^cite_note-FT14-352] In 2015, [Reuters](18998750.md) wrote that BP is "known for their more liberal policies for gay and transgender workers".[^cite_note-Driver15-353] A 2016 article in the *Houston Chronicle* said BP was "among the first major companies in the United States to offer LGBT workers equal protection and benefits roughly 20 years ago".[^cite_note-354] BP scored a 100% on the 2018 Human Rights Campaign's Corporate Equality Index, which was released in 2017, although this was the most common score.[^cite_note-EqualityIndex18-355] Also in 2017, BP added gender reassignment surgery to its list of benefits for U.S. employees.[^cite_note-Hunn17-356] According to the Human Rights Campaign, BP is one of only a few oil and gas companies offering transgender benefits to its employees.[^cite_note-Hunn17-356] BP ranked No. 51 on the list of Top 100 employers for lesbian, gay, bisexual and transgender staff on the 2017 Stonewall Workplace Equality Index.[^cite_note-SWEI-357] Also in 2017, John Mingé, chairman and president of BP America, signed a letter alongside other Houston oil executives denouncing the proposed "bathroom bill" in Texas.[^cite_note-Andrews17-358] [Bobbi Pickard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobbi_Pickard) has praised the company's LGBTQ inclusivity, stating that coming out as transgender while working with company in 2017 "saved her life".[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]

## Environmental record

### Climate policy

Prior to 1997, BP was a member of the [Global Climate Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Climate_Coalition), an industry organisation established to promote [global warming scepticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming_scepticism), but withdrew in 1997, saying "the time to consider the policy dimensions of [climate change](5042951.md) is not when the link between [greenhouse gases](21350772.md) and climate change is conclusively proven, but when the possibility cannot be discounted and is taken seriously by the society of which we are part. We in BP have reached that point.".[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-Stanford_GSB-362] BP was distinguished as the first multinational outside of the reinsurance industry to publicly support the [scientific consensus on climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_consensus_on_climate_change), which [Pew Center on Global Climate Change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Center_on_Global_Climate_Change) president [Eileen Claussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eileen_Claussen) then described as a transformative moment on the issue.[^cite_note-363] In March 2002, Lord John Browne, the group chief executive of BP that time, declared in a speech that [global warming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming) was real and that urgent action was needed.[^cite_note-nyt050610-364] Notwithstanding this, from 1988 to 2015 BP was responsible for 1.53% of global industrial greenhouse gas emissions.[^cite_note-guardian100717-365] In 2015, BP was listed by the UK-based non-profit organisation Influence Map as the fiercest opponent of action on climate change in Europe.[^cite_note-guardian050610-366] In 2018, BP was the largest contributor to the campaign opposing [carbon fee initiative 1631](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Initiative_1631) in Washington State.[^cite_note-367] Robert Allendorfer, manager of BP's Cherry Point refinery, wrote the following in a letter to state lawmakers: "\[Initiative 1631] would exempt six of the ten largest stationary source emitters in the state, including a coal-fired power plant, an aluminum smelter, and a number of pulp and paper plants."[^cite_note-368] According to a 2019 [Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian) ranking, BP was the 6th largest emitter of greenhouse gases in the world.[^cite_note-guardian-369]

In February 2020, BP set a goal to cut its greenhouse gas emissions to net-zero by 2050. BP is seeking [net-zero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_neutrality) [carbon emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_emissions) across its operations and the fuels the company sells, including emissions from cars, homes, and factories.[^cite_note-LooneyCEO-156][^cite_note-LAT-Emissions-370][^cite_note-guardian120220-371] Details on the scope of this and how this will be achieved are publicly limited.[^cite_note-372] BP said that it is restructuring its operations into four business groups to meet these goals: production and operations; customers and products; gas and low carbon; and innovation and engineering.[^cite_note-LooneyCEO-156] The company discontinued involvement with [American Fuel and Petrochemical Manufacturers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Fuel_and_Petrochemical_Manufacturers), [Western States Petroleum Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_States_Petroleum_Association), and Western Energy Alliance, involved in lobbying government within the United States, because of differences of position on the issue of methane and carbon policies, as a development of this new commitment.[^cite_note-sky260220-373][^cite_note-bloomberg260220-374] However, an investigation conducted by Unearthed, an investigations unit of [Greenpeace UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenpeace_UK), and [HuffPost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HuffPost) unveiled eight anti-climate trade associations BP failed to disclose, including Alliance of Western Energy Consumers, [Texas Oil and Gas Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Oil_and_Gas_Association), [Australian Petroleum Production and Exploration Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Petroleum_Production_and_Exploration_Association), and the [Business Council of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Council_of_Australia), among others.[^cite_note-huffpost280920-375]

In August 2020, BP America's chairman David Lawler criticised elimination of federal requirements to install equipment to detect and fix [methane leaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methane_leaks) by saying that "direct federal regulation of [methane emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methane_emissions) is essential to preventing leaks throughout the industry and protecting the environment."[^cite_note-376] BP is also a founding member of Methane Guiding Principles, an industry consortium that aims to drive methane reductions throughout the global energy supply chain.[^cite_note-377]

In BP's Energy Outlook 2020, BP stated that the changing energy landscape coupled with the [economic toll of the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_market_impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic#Oil_prices) means that the global crude demand [will never again surpass 2019's average](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peak_oil). All three scenarios in the outlook see the consumption of coal, oil, and natural gas dropping while the role of renewable energy will soar. BP was also attempting to move from being an international oil company into becoming an integrated energy company that would focus on low-carbon technologies while also setting a target to reduce its overall oil and gas production by 40% by 2030.[^cite_note-378] This plan was scrapped in October 2024, with BP looking to increase its oil and gas production instead.[^cite_note-\:4-288]

In 2021, BP was ranked as the 5th most environmentally responsible company out of 120 oil, gas, and mining companies involved in resource extraction north of the Arctic Circle in the Arctic Environmental Responsibility Index (AERI).[^cite_note-379]

In December 2022, [U.S. House Oversight and Reform Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Oversight_and_Reform) Chair [Carolyn Maloney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolyn_Maloney) and [U.S. House Oversight Environment Subcommittee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Oversight_Subcommittee_on_Environment) Chair [Ro Khanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ro_Khanna) sent a memorandum to all House Oversight and Reform Committee members summarizing additional findings from the Committee's investigation into the fossil fuel industry disinformation campaign to obscure the role of fossil fuels in causing global warming, and that upon reviewing internal company documents, accused BP along with [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil), [Chevron](284749.md), and [Shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_plc) of [greenwashing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwashing) their [Paris Agreement](30242372.md) [carbon neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_neutrality) pledges while continuing long-term investment in fossil fuel production and sales, for engaging in a campaign to promote the use of natural gas as a clean energy source and bridge fuel to renewable energy, and of intimidating journalists reporting about the companies' climate actions and of obstructing the Committee's investigation.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382]

After initially pledging to reduce its emissions by 35% by 2030, BP stated in 2023 that it would aim for a 20-30% reduction instead.[^cite_note-383] Following pressure to boost profits from a group of shareholders including [Elliott Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott_Investment_Management), in February 2025 the company announced plans to significantly reduce its investment in renewable energy, whilst increasing that in oil and gas production by 20%. Some other shareholders raised concerns over the enhanced focus on fossil fuels, which was also criticised by various environmental groups.[^cite_note-384]

### Indigenous rights

In a 2016 study, conducted by [Indra Øverland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indra_%C3%98verland) of [Norwegian Institute of International Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Institute_of_International_Affairs) BP was ranked 15th out of 18 levels (in total 37th out of 92 oil, gas and mining companies) on indigenous rights and resource extraction in the Arctic. The ranking of companies took into account 20 criteria, such as the companies' commitments to international standards, the presence of organisational units dedicated to handling indigenous rights, competent staffing, track records on indigenous issues, transparency, and procedures for consulting with indigenous peoples, but the actual performance of companies on indigenous rights was not assessed.[^cite_note-385]

### Hazardous substance dumping 1993–1995

In September 1999, one of BP's US subsidiaries, BP Exploration Alaska (BPXA), pleaded guilty to criminal charges stemming from its illegally dumping of hazardous wastes on the [Alaska North Slope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_North_Slope), paying fines and penalties totalling \$22 million. BP paid the maximum \$500,000 in criminal fines, \$6.5 million in civil penalties, and established a \$15 million environmental management system at all of BP facilities in the US and Gulf of Mexico that are engaged in oil exploration, drilling or production. The charges stemmed from the 1993 to 1995 dumping of hazardous wastes on [Endicott Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endicott_Island), Alaska by BP's contractor Doyon Drilling. The firm illegally discharged [waste oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_oil), paint thinner and other toxic and hazardous substances by injecting them down the outer rim, or annuli, of the oil wells. BPXA failed to report the illegal injections when it learned of the conduct, in violation of the [Comprehensive Environmental Response, Compensation and Liability Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Environmental_Response,_Compensation_and_Liability_Act).[^cite_note-386]

### Air pollution violations

In 2000, BP Amoco acquired ARCO, a Los Angeles-based oil group.[^cite_note-independent.co.uk-85] In 2003, California's [South Coast Air Quality Management District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Coast_Air_Quality_Management_District) (AQMD) filed a complaint against BP/ARCO, seeking \$319 million in penalties for thousands of air pollution violations over an 8-year period.[^cite_note-387] In January 2005, the agency filed a second suit against BP based on violations between August 2002 and October 2004. The suit alleged that BP illegally released air pollutants by failing to adequately inspect, maintain, repair and properly operate thousands of pieces of equipment across the refinery as required by AQMD regulations. It was alleged that in some cases the violations were due to negligence, while in others the violations were knowingly and willfully committed by refinery officials.[^cite_note-388] In 2005, a settlement was reached under which BP agreed to pay \$25 million in cash penalties and \$6 million in past emissions fees, while spending \$20 million on environmental improvements at the refinery and \$30 million on community programs focused on asthma diagnosis and treatment.[^cite_note-389]

In 2013, a total of 474 [Galveston County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galveston_County) residents living near the BP Texas City Refinery filed a \$1 billion lawsuit against BP, accusing the company of "intentionally misleading the public about the seriousness" of a two-week release of toxic fumes which began on 10 November 2011. "BP reportedly released Sulfur Dioxide, Methyl Carpaptan, Dimethyl Disulfide and other toxic chemicals into the atmosphere" reads the report. The lawsuit further claims Galveston county has the worst air quality in the United States due to BP's violations of air pollution laws. BP had no comment and said it would address the suit in the court system.[^cite_note-Courthouse-news-4-15-13-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-Rice-April162013-392][^cite_note-393]

### Colombian farmland damages claim

In 2006, a group of [Colombian](5222.md) farmers reached a multimillion-dollar out-of-court settlement with BP for alleged environmental damage caused by the [Ocensa pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocensa_pipeline).[^cite_note-394] The company was accused of benefiting from a regime of terror carried out by Colombian government paramilitaries to protect the 450-mile (720 km) Ocensa pipeline; BP said throughout that it has acted responsibly and that landowners were fairly compensated.[^cite_note-395]

In 2009, another group of 95 Colombian farmers filed a suit against BP, saying the company's Ocensa pipeline caused landslides and damage to soil and groundwater, affecting crops, livestock, and contaminating water supplies, making fish ponds unsustainable. Most of the land traversed by the pipeline was owned by peasant farmers who were illiterate and unable to read the environmental impact assessment conducted by BP prior to construction, which acknowledged significant and widespread risks of damage to the land.[^cite_note-396] The [Supreme Court of Justice of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Justice_of_Colombia) handed down a judgement rejecting the case in August 2016.[^cite_note-397]

### Canadian oil sands

See also: [List of articles about Canadian oil sands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_articles_about_Canadian_oil_sands)

Since 2007, BP has been involved in oil sands projects,[^cite_note-guardian061207-398] which Greenpeace has called a climate crime.[^cite_note-guardian071207-399] Members of Canada's [First Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Nations_in_Canada) have criticised BP's involvement for the impacts oil sands extraction has on the environment.[^cite_note-Macalister2009-400] In 2010, BP pledged to use only *in-situ* technologies instead of open-pit mining.[^cite_note-guardian150410-401] It uses [steam-assisted gravity drainage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steam-assisted_gravity_drainage) *in-situ* technology to extract [bitumen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitumen).[^cite_note-ReutersJan2010-402] According to Greenpeace it is even more damaging to climate because while according to the [Pembina Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pembina_Institute) *in-situ* techniques result in lower [nitrogen oxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitrogen_oxide) emissions, and are less damaging to the landscape and rivers, they cause more greenhouse gas and [sulphur dioxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulphur_dioxide) emissions than mining.[^cite_note-guardian150410-401] In 2010, activist shareholders asked BP for a full investigation of the Sunrise oil sands project, but were defeated.[^cite_note-guardian150410-401][^cite_note-wsj150410-403] In 2013, shareholders criticised the project for being [carbon-intensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emission_intensity).[^cite_note-guardian100413-404]

## Violations and accidents

Citing conditions similar to those that resulted in the 2005 [Texas City refinery explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_City_refinery_explosion), on 25 April 2006, the U.S. Department of Labor's [Occupational Safety and Health Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_Safety_and_Health_Administration) (OSHA) fined BP more than \$2.4 million for unsafe operations at the company's [Oregon, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oregon,_Ohio) refinery. An OSHA inspection resulted in 32 per-instance wilful citations including locating people in vulnerable buildings among the processing units, failing to correct depressurisation deficiencies and deficiencies with gas monitors, and failing to prevent the use of non-approved electrical equipment in locations in which hazardous concentrations of flammable gases or vapours may exist. BP was further fined for neglecting to develop shutdown procedures and designate responsibilities and to establish a system to promptly address and resolve recommendations made after an incident when a large feed pump failed three years prior to 2006. Penalties were also issued for five serious violations, including failure to develop operating procedures for a unit that removes sulphur compound; failure to ensure that operating procedures reflect current operating practice in the Isocracker Unit; failure to resolve process hazard analysis recommendations; failure to resolve process safety management compliance audit items in a timely manner; and failure to periodically inspect pressure piping systems.[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-CNN-4-25-06-406]

In 2008, BP and several other major oil refiners agreed to pay \$422 million to settle a class-action lawsuit stemming from water contamination tied to the gasoline additive [MTBE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTBE), a chemical that was once a key gasoline ingredient. Leaked from storage tanks, MTBE has been found in several water systems across the United States. The plaintiffs maintain that the industry knew about the environmental dangers but that they used it instead of other possible alternatives because it was less expensive. The companies will also be required to pay 70% of cleanup costs for any wells newly affected at any time over the next 30 years.[^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408]

BP has one of the worst safety records of any major oil company that operates in the United States. Between 2007 and 2010, BP refineries in Ohio and Texas accounted for 97% of "egregious, willful" violations handed out by the U.S. Occupational Safety and Health Administration (OSHA). BP had 760 "egregious, willful" violations during that period, while Sunoco and Conoco-Phillips each had eight, Citgo two and Exxon had one.[^cite_note-ABC-5-2010-409] The deputy assistant secretary of labour at OSHA, said "The only thing you can conclude is that BP has a serious, systemic safety problem in their company."[^cite_note-410]

A report in *[ProPublica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ProPublica)*, published in *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* in 2010, found that over a decade of internal investigations of BP's Alaska operations during the 2000s warned senior BP managers that the company repeatedly disregarded safety and environmental rules and risked a serious accident if it did not change its ways. ProPublica found that "Taken together, these documents portray a company that systemically ignored its own safety policies across its North American operations – from Alaska to the Gulf of Mexico to California and Texas. Executives were not held accountable for the failures, and some were promoted despite them."[^cite_note-Lustgarten-6-2010-411]

The [Project On Government Oversight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_On_Government_Oversight), an independent non-profit organisation in the United States which investigates and seeks to expose corruption and other misconduct, lists BP as number one on their listing of the 100 worst corporations based on instances of misconduct.[^cite_note-412]

### 1965 Sea Gem offshore oil rig disaster

Main article: [Sea Gem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Gem)

In December 1965, Britain's first oil rig, Sea Gem, capsized when two of the legs collapsed during an operation to move it to a new location. The oil rig had been hastily converted in an effort to quickly start drilling operations after the [North Sea](21179.md) was opened for exploration. Thirteen crew members were killed. No hydrocarbons were released in the accident.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-dukeswood-414]

### Texas City refinery explosion and leaks

The former [Amoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoco) oil refinery at [Texas City, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_City,_Texas), was beset by environmental issues, including chemical leaks and a 2005 explosion that killed 15 people and injured hundreds. *[Bloomberg News](3057255.md)* described the incident, which led to a guilty plea by BP to a felony [Clean Air Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_Air_Act_\(United_States\)) charge, as "one of the deadliest U.S. industrial accidents in 20 years." The refinery was sold to [Marathon Petroleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathon_Petroleum) in October 2012.[^cite_note-Bloomberg-Texas_City_refinery_sale-415]

#### 2005 explosion

Main article: [Texas City refinery explosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_City_refinery_explosion)



Fire-extinguishing operations after the Texas City refinery explosion

In March 2005, the Texas City refinery, one of the largest refineries owned then by BP, exploded causing 15 deaths, injuring 180 people and forcing thousands of nearby residents to remain sheltered in their homes.[^cite_note-McClatchy_Washington_Bureau-416] A 20-foot (6.1 m) column filled with hydrocarbon overflowed to form a vapour cloud, which ignited. The explosion caused all the casualties and substantial damage to the rest of the plant.[^cite_note-NYT_12_July_2010-417] The incident came as the culmination of a series of less serious accidents at the refinery, and the engineering problems were not addressed by the management. Maintenance and safety at the plant had been cut as a cost-saving measure, the responsibility ultimately resting with executives in London.[^cite_note-baker-418]

The fallout from the accident clouded BP's corporate image because of the mismanagement at the plant. There had been several investigations of the disaster, the most recent being that from the [US Chemical Safety and Hazard Investigation Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Chemical_Safety_and_Hazard_Investigation_Board)[^cite_note-419] which "offered a scathing assessment of the company." OSHA found "organizational and safety deficiencies at all levels of the BP Corporation" and said management failures could be traced from Texas to London.[^cite_note-McClatchy_Washington_Bureau-416] The company pleaded guilty to a [felony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felony) violation of the [Clean Air Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_Air_Act_\(United_States\)), was fined \$50 million, the largest ever assessed under the Clean Air Act, and sentenced to three years probation.[^cite_note-justice.gov-420]

On 30 October 2009, the US [Occupational Safety and Health Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupational_Safety_and_Health_Administration) (OSHA) fined BP an additional \$87 million, the largest fine in OSHA history, for failing to correct safety hazards documented in the 2005 explosion. Inspectors found 270 safety violations that had been cited but not fixed and 439 new violations. BP appealed the fine.[^cite_note-McClatchy_Washington_Bureau-416][^cite_note-421] In July 2012, the company agreed to pay \$13 million to settle the new violations. At that time OSHA found "no imminent dangers" at the Texas plant. Thirty violations remained under discussion.[^cite_note-reuters120712-422] In March 2012, US Department of Justice officials said the company had met all of its obligations and subsequently ended the probationary period.[^cite_note-423] In November 2011, BP agreed to pay the state of Texas \$50 million for violating state emissions standards at its Texas City refinery during and after the 2005 explosion at the refinery. The state Attorney General said BP was responsible for 72 separate pollutant emissions that have been occurring every few months since March 2005. It was the largest fine ever imposed under the Texas Clean Air Act.[^cite_note-Florian-4-13-424][^cite_note-ChronNov32011-425]

#### 2007 toxic substance release

In 2007, 143 workers at the Texas City refinery claimed that they were injured when a toxic substance was released at the plant. In December 2009, after a three-week trial, a federal jury in Galveston awarded ten of those workers \$10 million each in punitive damages, in addition to smaller damages for medical expenses and pain and suffering. The plant had a history of chemical releases.[^cite_note-Times_-_AP_-_Texas_refinery_-_2009-426] In March 2010, the federal judge hearing the case reduced the jury's award to less than \$500,000. U.S. District Judge Kenneth M. Hoyt said the plaintiffs failed to prove BP was grossly negligent.[^cite_note-Chron_-_2010_Texas_City-427]

#### 2010 chemical leak

In August 2010, the Texas Attorney General charged BP with illegally emitting harmful air pollutants from its [Texas City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_City) refinery for more than a month. BP has admitted that malfunctioning equipment led to the release of over 530,000 pounds (240,000 kg) of chemicals into the air of Texas City and surrounding areas from 6 April to 16 May 2010. The leak included 17,000 pounds (7,700 kg) of [benzene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benzene), 37,000 pounds (17,000 kg) of [nitrogen oxides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitrogen_oxides), and 186,000 pounds (84,000 kg) of [carbon monoxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_monoxide). The State's investigation showed that BP's failure to properly maintain its equipment caused the malfunction. When the equipment malfunctioned and caught fire, BP workers shut it down and routed escaping gases to flares. Rather than shut down associated units while compressor repairs were made, BP chose to keep operating those other units, which led to unlawful release of contaminants for almost 40 days. The Attorney General is seeking civil penalties of no less than \$50 nor greater than \$25,000 per day of each violation of state air quality laws, as well as attorneys' fees and investigative costs.[^cite_note-ChronNov32011-425][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-The_Attorney_General_of_Texas-429]

In June 2012, over 50,000 Texas City residents joined a class-action suit against BP, alleging they became sick in 2010 as a result of the emissions release from the refinery. BP said the release harmed no one.[^cite_note-430] In October 2013, a trial designed as a test for a larger suit that includes 45,000 people found that BP was negligent in the case, but due to the lack of substantial evidence linking illness to the emissions, decided the company would be absolved of any wrongdoing.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432]

### Prudhoe Bay

Main article: [Prudhoe Bay oil spill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudhoe_Bay_oil_spill)



Aerial view of Prudhoe Bay

In March 2006, corrosion of a BP Exploration Alaska (BPXA) oil transit pipeline in [Prudhoe Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudhoe_Bay) transporting oil to the [Trans-Alaska Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System) led to a five-day leak and the largest oil spill on [Alaska's North Slope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_North_Slope).[^cite_note-Roach2006-9] According to the Alaska Department of Environmental Conservation (ADEC), a total of 212,252 US gallons (5,053.6 bbl; 803.46 m<sup>3</sup>) of oil was spilled, covering 2 acres (0.81 ha) of the North Slope.[^cite_note-ADECMar2008-433] BP admitted that cost-cutting measures had resulted in a lapse in monitoring and maintenance of the pipeline and the consequent leak. At the moment of the leak, [pipeline inspection gauges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pipeline_inspection_gauges) (known as "pigs") had not been run through the pipeline since 1998.[^cite_note-Rosen2007-434][^cite_note-Baltimore2007-435][^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437] BP completed the clean-up of the spill by May 2006, including removal of contaminated gravel and vegetation, which was replaced with new material from the Arctic tundra.[^cite_note-ADECMar2008-433][^cite_note-Benschoten2006-438]

Following the spill, the company was ordered by regulators to inspect the 35 kilometres (22 mi) of pipelines in Prudhoe Bay using "smart pigs".[^cite_note-Kleiner2006-439] In late July 2006, the "smart pigs" monitoring the pipelines found 16 places where corrosion had thinned pipeline walls. A BP crew sent to inspect the pipe in early August discovered a leak and small spill,[^cite_note-Kleiner2006-439][^cite_note-Isidore2006-440] following which, BP announced that the eastern portion of the Alaskan field would be shut down for repairs on the pipeline,[^cite_note-Isidore2006-440][^cite_note-Zibel2007-441] with approval from the [Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Transportation). The shutdown resulted in a reduction of 200,000 barrels per day (32,000 m<sup>3</sup>/d) until work began to bring the eastern field to full production on 2 October 2006.[^cite_note-Alaska_Update-442] In total, 23 barrels (3.7 m<sup>3</sup>) of oil were spilled and 176 barrels (28.0 m<sup>3</sup>) were "contained and recovered", according to ADEC. The spill was cleaned up and there was no impact upon wildlife.[^cite_note-ADECAug2006-443]

After the shutdown, BP pledged to replace 26 kilometres (16 mi) of its Alaskan oil transit pipelines[^cite_note-Nelson2007-444][^cite_note-HeraldMar2008-445] and the company completed work on the 16 miles (26 km) of new pipeline by the end of 2008.[^cite_note-BPPrudhoeBayFact-446] In November 2007, BP Exploration, Alaska pleaded guilty to negligent discharge of oil, a misdemeanour under the federal [Clean Water Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_Water_Act) and was fined US\$20 million.[^cite_note-corrosion-447] There was no charge brought for the smaller spill in August 2006 due to BP's quick response and clean-up.[^cite_note-Rosen2007-434] On 16 October 2007, ADEC officials reported a "toxic spill" from a BP pipeline in Prudhoe Bay comprising 2,000 US gallons (7,600 L; 1,700 imp gal) of primarily [methanol (methyl alcohol)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methanol) mixed with crude oil and water, which spilled onto a gravel pad and frozen tundra pond.[^cite_note-ktuu-448]

In the settlement of a civil suit, in July 2011 investigators from the U.S. Department of Transportation's Pipeline and Hazardous Materials Safety Administration determined that the 2006 spills were a result of BPXA's failure to properly inspect and maintain the pipeline to prevent corrosion. The government issued a Corrective Action Order to BP XA that addressed the pipeline's risks and ordered pipeline repair or replacement. The U.S. Environmental Protection Agency had investigated the extent of the oil spills and oversaw BPXA's cleanup. When BP XA did not fully comply with the terms of the corrective action, a complaint was filed in March 2009 alleging violations of the Clean Water Act, the Clean Air Act and the Pipeline Safety Act. In July 2011, the U.S. District Court for the District of Alaska entered a consent decree between the United States and BPXA resolving the government's claims. Under the consent decree, BPXA paid a \$25 million civil penalty, the largest per-barrel penalty at that time for an oil spill, and agreed to take measures to significantly improve inspection and maintenance of its pipeline infrastructure on the North Slope to reduce the threat of additional oil spills.[^cite_note-autogenerated1-449][^cite_note-APMay32011-450]

### 2008 Caspian Sea gas leak

On 17 September 2008, a small gas leak was discovered and one gas-injection well broached to surface in the area of the Central Azeri platform at the [Azeri oilfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azeri_oilfield), a part of the [Azeri–Chirag–Guneshli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azeri%E2%80%93Chirag%E2%80%93Guneshli) (ACG) project, in the Azerbaijan sector of [Caspian Sea](19653787.md).[^cite_note-reuters170908-451][^cite_note-time181210-452] The platform was shut down and the staff was evacuated.[^cite_note-reuters170908-451] As the West Azeri Platform was being powered by a cable from the Central Azeri Platform, it was also shut down.[^cite_note-wikileaks081008-453] Production at the West Azeri Platform resumed on 9 October 2008 and at the Central Azeri Platform in December 2008.[^cite_note-reuters101008-454][^cite_note-reuters231208-455] According to leaked US Embassy cables, BP had been "exceptionally circumspect in disseminating information" and showed that BP thought the cause for the blowout was a bad cement job. The cables further said that some of BP's ACG partners complained that the company was so secretive that it was withholding information even from them.[^cite_note-time181210-452][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-wikileaks150109-457]

### California storage tanks

Santa Barbara County District Attorney sued BP West Coast Products LLC, BP Products North America, Inc., and Atlantic Richfield Company over allegations that the companies violated state laws regarding operating and maintaining motor vehicle fuel underground storage tank laws. BP settled a lawsuit for \$14 million. The complaint alleged that BP failed to properly inspect and maintain underground tanks used to store gasoline for retail sale at approximately 780 gas stations in California over a period of ten years and violated other hazardous material and hazardous waste laws. The case settled in November 2016 and was the result of collaboration among the California Attorney General's Office and several district attorney's offices across the state.[^cite_note-458]
